> <tr><td rowspan="9">1B001</td><td>Equipment for the production or inspection of “composite” structures or laminates specified in 1A002 or “fibrous or filamentary materials” specified in 1C010, as follows, and specially designed components and accessories therefor:<br><strong>N.B.:</strong><br><strong>SEE ALSO 1B101 AND 1B201.</strong></td><td></td><td></td></tr>
> <tr><td>a.<br>Filament winding machines, of which the motions for positioning, wrapping and winding fibres are coordinated and programmed in three or more ‘primary servo positioning’ axes, specially designed for the manufacture of “composite” structures or laminates, from “fibrous or filamentary materials”;</td><td>M6B1a</td><td>Filament winding machines or ‘fibre/tow-placement machines’, of which the motions for positioning, wrapping and winding fibres can be coordinated and programmed in three or more axes, designed to fabricate composite structures or laminates from fibrous or filamentary materials, and co-ordinating and programming controls</td></tr>
> <tr><td>b.<br>‘Tape-laying machines’, of which the motions for positioning and laying tape are coordinated and programmed in five or more ‘primary servo positioning’ axes, specially designed for the manufacture of “composite” airframe or ‘missile’ structures;<br><em>Note:</em><br><em>In 1B001.b., ‘missile’ means complete rocket systems and unmanned aerial vehicle systems.</em><br><em>Technical Note:</em><br><em>For the purposes of 1B001.b., ‘tape-laying machines’ have the ability to lay one or more ‘filament bands’ limited to widths greater than 25 mm and less than or equal to 305 mm, and to cut and restart individual ‘filament band’ courses during the laying process.</em></td><td>M6B1b</td><td>‘Tape-laying machines’ of which the motions for positioning and laying tape can be co-ordinated and programmed in two or more axes, designed for the manufacture of composite airframes and missile structures;<br><em>Note:</em><br><em>For the purposes of 6.B.1.a. and 6.B.1.b., the following definitions apply:</em><br>1.. <em>A ‘filament band’ is a single continuous width of fully or partially resinimpregnated tape, tow, or fibre. Fully or partially resin-impregnated ‘filament bands’ include those coated with dry powder that tacks upon heating.</em><br>2.. <em>‘Fibre/tow-placement machines’ and ‘tape-laying machines’ are machines that perform similar processes that use computer-guided heads to lay one or several ‘filament bands’ onto a mold to create a part or a structure. These machines have the ability to cut and restart individual ‘filament band’ courses during the laying process.</em><br>3.. <em>‘Fibre/tow-placement machines’ have the ability to place one or more ‘filament bands’ having widths less than or equal to 25,4 mm. This refers to the minimum width of material the machine can place, regardless of the upper capability of the machine.</em><br>4.. <em>‘Tape-laying machines’ have the ability to place one or more ‘filament bands’ having widths less than or equal to 304,8 mm, but cannot place ‘filaments bands’ with a width equal to or less than 25,4 mm. This refers to the minimum width of material the machine can place, regardless of the upper capability of the machine.</em></td></tr>
> <tr><td>c.<br>Multidirectional, multidimensional weaving machines or interlacing machines, including adapters and modification kits, specially designed or modified for weaving, interlacing or braiding fibres, for “composite” structures;<br><em>Technical Note:</em><br><em>For the purposes of 1B001.c., the technique of interlacing includes knitting.</em></td><td>M6B1c</td><td>Multi-directional, multi-dimensional weaving machines or interlacing machines, including adapters and modification kits for weaving, interlacing or braiding fibres to manufacture composite structures;<br><em>Note:</em><br><em>6.B.1.c. does not control textile machinery not modified for the end-uses stated.</em></td></tr>
> <tr><td>d.<br>Equipment specially designed or adapted for the production of reinforcement fibres, as follows:</td><td></td><td>Equipment designed or modified for the production of fibrous or filamentary materials as follows:</td></tr>
> <tr><td>1.<br>Equipment for converting polymeric fibres (such as polyacrylonitrile, rayon, pitch or polycarbosilane) into carbon fibres or silicon carbide fibres, including special equipment to strain the fibre during heating;</td><td>M6B1d1</td><td>1.<br>Equipment for converting polymeric fibres (such as polyacrylonitrile, rayon, or polycarbosilane) including special provision to strain the fibre during heating;</td></tr>
> <tr><td>2.<br>Equipment for the chemical vapour deposition of elements or compounds, on heated filamentary substrates, to manufacture silicon carbide fibres;</td><td>M6B1d2</td><td>2.<br>Equipment for the vapour deposition of elements or compounds on heated filament substrates;</td></tr>
> <tr><td>3.<br>Equipment for the wet-spinning of refractory ceramics (such as aluminium oxide);</td><td>M6B1d3</td><td>3.<br>Equipment for the wet-spinning of refractory ceramics (such as aluminium oxide)</td></tr>
> <tr><td>4.<br>Equipment for converting aluminium containing precursor fibres into alumina fibres by heat treatment;<br>e.<br>Equipment for producing prepregs specified in 1C010.e. by the hot melt method;<br>f.<br>Non-destructive inspection equipment specially designed for “composite” materials, as follows:<br>1.. X-ray tomography systems for three dimensional defect inspection;<br>2.. Numerically controlled ultrasonic testing machines of which the motions for positioning transmitters or receivers are simultaneously coordinated and programmed in four or more axes to follow the three dimensional contours of the component under inspection;<br>g.<br>‘Tow-placement machines’, of which the motions for positioning and laying tows are coordinated and programmed in two or more ‘primary servo positioning’ axes, specially designed for the manufacture of “composite” airframe or ‘missile’ structures.<br><em>Technical Note:</em><br><em>For the purposes of 1B001.g., ‘tow-placement machines’ have the ability to place one or more ‘filament bands’ having widths less than or equal to 25 mm, and to cut and restart individual ‘filament band’ courses during the placement process.</em><br><em>Technical Note:</em><br><em>1.</em><br><em>For the purpose of 1B001, ‘primary servo positioning’ axes control, under computer program direction, the position of the end effector (i.e., head) in space relative to the work piece at the correct orientation and direction to achieve the desired process.</em><br><em>2.</em><br><em>For the purposes of 1B001., a ‘filament band’ is a single continuous width of fully or partially resin-impregnated tape, tow or fibre.</em></td><td>M6B1e</td><td>Equipment designed or modified for special fibre surface treatment or for producing prepregs and preforms, including rollers, tension stretchers, coating equipment, cutting equipment and clicker dies.<br><em>Note:</em><br><em>Examples of components and accessories for the machines specified in 6.B.1. are moulds, mandrels, dies, fixtures and tooling for the preform pressing, curing, casting, sintering or bonding of composite structures, laminates and manufactures thereof</em></td></tr>
> <tr><td>1B002</td><td>Equipment for producing metal alloys, metal alloy powder or alloyed materials, specially designed to avoid contamination and specially designed for use in one of the processes specified in 1C002.c.2.<br><strong>N.B.:</strong><br><strong>SEE ALSO 1B102.</strong></td><td>M4B3d</td><td>Metal powder “production equipment” usable for the “production”, in a controlled environment, of spherical, spheroidal or atomised materials specified in 4.C.2.c., 4.C.2.d. or 4.C.2.e. Note: 4.B.3.d. includes: a. Plasma generators (high frequency arc-jet) usable for obtaining sputtered or spherical metallic powders with organization of the process in an argon-water environment; b. Electroburst equipment usable for obtaining sputtered or spherical metallic powders with organization of the process in an argon-water environment; c. Equipment usable for the “production” of spherical aluminium powders by powdering a melt in an inert medium (e.g. nitrogen).<br>Notes:<br>1.<br>The only batch mixers, continuous mixers, usable for solid propellants or propellants constituents specified in 4.C., and fluid energy mills specified in 4.B., are those specified in 4.B.3.<br>2.<br>Forms of metal powder “production equipment” not specified in 4.B.3.d. are to be evaluated in accordance with 4.B.2.</td></tr>
> <tr><td rowspan="5">1B101</td><td>Equipment, other than that specified in 1B001, for the “production” of structural composites as follows; and specially designed components and accessories therefor:<br><strong>N.B.:</strong><br><strong>SEE ALSO 1B201.</strong><br>Note:<br>Components and accessories specified in 1B101 include moulds, mandrels, dies, fixtures and tooling for the preform pressing, curing, casting, sintering or bonding of composite structures, laminates and manufactures thereof.</td><td></td><td></td></tr>

These machines have the ability to cut and restart individual ‘filament band’ courses during the laying process.</em><br>3.. <em>‘Fibre/tow-placement machines’ have the ability to place one or more ‘filament bands’ having widths less than or equal to 25,4 mm. > <tr><td>a.<br>“Production equipment” for the “production”, handling or acceptance testing of liquid propellants or propellant constituents specified in 1C011.a., 1C011.b., 1C111 or in the Military Goods Controls;</td><td>M4B1</td><td>“Production equipment”, and specially designed components therefor, for the “production”, handling or acceptance testing of liquid propellants or propellant constituents specified in 4.C.</td></tr>
> <tr><td>b.<br>“Production equipment” for the “production”, handling, mixing, curing, casting, pressing, machining, extruding or acceptance testing of solid propellants or propellant constituents specified in 1C011.a., 1C011.b., 1C111 or in the Military Goods Controls.<br><em>Note:</em><br><em>1B115.b. does not control batch mixers, continuous mixers or fluid energy mills. For the control of batch mixers, continuous mixers and fluid energy mills see 1B117, 1B118 and 1B119.</em><br><em>Note 1:</em><br><em>For equipment specially designed for the production of military goods, see the Military Goods Controls.</em><br><em>Note 2:</em><br><em>1B115 does not control equipment for the “production”, handling and acceptance testing of boron carbide.</em></td><td>M4B2</td><td>“Production equipment”, other than that described in 4.B.3., and specially designed components therefor, for the production, handling, mixing, curing, casting, pressing, machining, extruding or acceptance testing of solid propellants or propellant constituents specified in 4.C.</td></tr>

> **1C**
>
> **Materials**
>
> <table>
> <tbody>
> <tr><th colspan="2">The corresponding systems, equipment and components as identified in Council Regulation (EC) No 428/2009 of 5 May 2009 setting up a Community regime for the control of exports, transfer, brokering and transit of dual-use items</th><th colspan="2">Missile Technology Control Regime (M.TCR): Equipment, software and technology annex</th></tr>
> <tr><td>1C001</td><td>Materials specially designed for use as absorbers of electromagnetic waves, or intrinsically conductive polymers, as follows:<br>N.B.:<br>SEE ALSO 1C101.<br>a.. Materials for absorbing frequencies exceeding 2 × 10<sup>8</sup> Hz but less than 3 × 10<sup>12</sup> Hz;<br><em>Note 1:</em><br><em>1C001.a. does not control:</em><br>a.. <em>Hair type absorbers, constructed of natural or synthetic fibres, with non-magnetic loading to provide absorption;</em><br>b.. <em>Absorbers having no magnetic loss and whose incident surface is non-planar in shape, including pyramids, cones, wedges and convoluted surfaces;</em><br>c.. <em>Planar absorbers, having all of the following:</em><br>1.. <em>Made from any of the following:</em><br>a.. <em>Plastic foam materials (flexible or non-flexible) with carbon-loading, or organic materials, including binders, providing more than 5 % echo compared with metal over a bandwidth exceeding ± 15 % of the centre frequency of the incident energy, and not capable of withstanding temperatures exceeding 450 K (177 °C); or</em><br>b.. <em>Ceramic materials providing more than 20 % echo compared with metal over a bandwidth exceeding ± 15 % of the centre frequency of the incident energy, and not capable of withstanding temperatures exceeding 800 K (527 °C);</em><br><em>Technical Note:</em><br><em>Absorption test samples for 1C001.a. Note: 1.c.1. should be a square at least 5 wavelengths of the centre frequency on a side and positioned in the far field of the radiating element.</em><br>2.. <em>Tensile strength less than 7 × 10<sup>6</sup> N/m<sup>2</sup>; and</em><br>3.. <em>Compressive strength less than 14 × 10<sup>6</sup> N/m<sup>2</sup>;</em><br>d.. <em>Planar absorbers made of sintered ferrite, having all of the following:</em><br>1.. <em>A specific gravity exceeding 4,4; and</em><br>2.. <em>A maximum operating temperature of 548 K (275 °C).</em><br><em>Note 2:</em><br><em>Nothing in Note 1 to 1C001.a. releases magnetic materials to provide absorption when contained in paint.</em><br>b.. Materials for absorbing frequencies exceeding 1,5 × 10<sup>14</sup> Hz but less than 3,7 × 10<sup>14</sup> Hz and not transparent to visible light;<br><em>Note:</em><br><em>1C001.b. does not control materials, specially designed or formulated for any of the following applications:</em><br>a.. <em>Laser marking of polymers; or</em><br>b.. <em>Laser welding of polymers.</em><br>c.. Intrinsically conductive polymeric materials with a ‘bulk electrical conductivity’ exceeding 10 000 S/m (Siemens per metre) or a ‘sheet (surface) resistivity’ of less than 100 ohms/square, based on any of the following polymers:<br>1.. Polyaniline;<br>2.. Polypyrrole;<br>3.. Polythiophene;<br>4.. Poly phenylene-vinylene; or<br>5.. Poly thienylene-vinylene.<br><em>Note:</em><br><em>1C001.c. does not control materials in a liquid form.</em><br><em>Technical Note:</em><br><em>‘Bulk electrical conductivity’ and ‘sheet (surface) resistivity’ should be determined using ASTM D-257 or national equivalents.</em></td><td>M17C1</td><td>Materials for reduced observables such as radar reflectivity, ultraviolet/infrared signatures and acoustic signatures (i.e. stealth technology), for applications usable for the systems specified in 1.A. or 19.A. or the subsystems specified in 2.A.<br><em>Notes:</em><br><em>1.</em><br><em>17.C.1. includes structural materials and coatings (including paints), specially designed for reduced or tailored reflectivity or emissivity in the microwave, infrared or ultraviolet spectra.</em><br><em>2.</em><br><em>17.C.1. does not control coatings (including paints) when specially used for thermal control of satellites.</em></td></tr>
> <tr><td rowspan="2">1C007</td><td>Ceramic powders, non-“composite” ceramic materials, ceramic-“matrix”“composite” materials and precursor materials, as follows:<br><strong>N.B.:</strong><br><strong>SEE ALSO 1C107.</strong></td><td>M6C5</td><td>Ceramic composite materials (dielectric constant less than 6 at any frequency from 100 MHz to 100 GHz) for use in missile radomes usable in systems specified in 1.A. or 19.A.1.</td></tr>
> <tr><td>a.. Ceramic powders of single or complex borides of titanium, having total metallic impurities, excluding intentional additions, of less than 5 000 ppm, an average particle size equal to or less than 5 μm and no more than 10 % of the particles larger than 10 μm;<br>b.. Non-“composite” ceramic materials in crude or semi-fabricated form, composed of borides of titanium with a density of 98 % or more of the theoretical density;<br><em>Note:</em><br><em>1C007.b. does not control abrasives.</em><br>c.. Ceramic-ceramic “composite” materials with a glass or oxide-“matrix” and reinforced with fibres having all of the following:<br>1.. Made from any of the following materials:<br>a.. Si-N;<br>b.. Si-C;<br>c.. Si-Al-O-N; or<br>d.. Si-O-N; and<br>2.. Having a “specific tensile strength” exceeding 12,7 × 10<sup>3</sup>m;<br>d.. Ceramic-ceramic “composite” materials, with or without a continuous metallic phase, incorporating particles, whiskers or fibres, where carbides or nitrides of silicon, zirconium or boron form the “matrix”;<br>e.. Precursor materials (i.e., special purpose polymeric or metallo-organic materials) for producing any phase or phases of the materials specified in 1C007.c., as follows:<br>1.. Polydiorganosilanes (for producing silicon carbide);<br>2.. Polysilazanes (for producing silicon nitride);<br>3.. Polycarbosilazanes (for producing ceramics with silicon, carbon and nitrogen components);<br>f.. Ceramic-ceramic “composite” materials with an oxide or glass “matrix” reinforced with continuous fibres from any of the following systems:<br>1.. Al<sub>2</sub>O<sub>3</sub> (CAS 1344-28-1); or<br>2.. Si-C-N.<br><em>Note:</em><br><em>1C007.f. does not control “composites” containing fibres from these systems with a fibre tensile strength of less than 700 MPa at 1 273 K (1 000 °C) or fibre tensile creep resistance of more than 1 % creep strain at 100 MPa load and 1 273 K (1 000 °C) for 100 hours.</em></td><td>M6C6</td><td>Silicon-carbide materials as follows:<br>a.. Bulk machinable silicon-carbide reinforced unfired ceramic usable for nose tips usable in systems specified in 1.A. or 19.A.1.;<br>Reinforced silicon-carbide ceramic composites usable for nose tips, re-entry vehicles, nozzle flaps, usable in systems specified in 1.A. or 19.A.1.</td></tr>
> <tr><td rowspan="2">1C010</td><td>“Fibrous or filamentary materials”, as follows:<br><strong>N.B.:</strong><br><strong>SEE ALSO 1C210 AND 9C110.</strong><br>a.. Organic “fibrous or filamentary materials”, having all of the following:<br>1.. “Specific modulus” exceeding 12,7 × 10<sup>6</sup> m; and<br>2.. “Specific tensile strength” exceeding 23,5 × 10<sup>4</sup> m;<br><em>Note:</em><br><em>1C010.a. does not control polyethylene.</em><br>b.. Carbon “fibrous or filamentary materials”, having all of the following:<br>1.. “Specific modulus” exceeding 14,65 × 10<sup>6</sup> m; and<br>2.. “Specific tensile strength” exceeding 26,82 × 10<sup>4</sup> m;<br><em>Note:</em><br><em>1C010.b. does not control:</em><br>a.. <em>“Fibrous or filamentary materials”, for the repair of “civil aircraft” structures or laminates, having all of the following:</em><br>1.. <em>An area not exceeding 1 m<sup>2</sup>;</em><br>2.. <em>A length not exceeding 2,5 m; and</em><br>3.. <em>A width exceeding 15 mm.</em><br>b.. <em>Mechanically chopped, milled or cut carbon “fibrous or filamentary materials” 25,0 mm or less in length.</em><br>c.. Inorganic “fibrous or filamentary materials”, having all of the following:<br>1.. “Specific modulus” exceeding 2,54 × 10<sup>6</sup> m; and<br>2.. Melting, softening, decomposition or sublimation point exceeding 1 922 K (1 649 °C) in an inert environment;<br><em>Note:</em><br><em>1C010.c. does not control:</em><br>a.. <em>Discontinuous, multiphase, polycrystalline alumina fibres in chopped fibre or random mat form, containing 3 % by weight or more silica, with a “specific modulus” of less than 10 × 10<sup>6</sup> m;</em><br>b.. <em>Molybdenum and molybdenum alloy fibres;</em><br>c.. <em>Boron fibres;</em><br>d.. <em>Discontinuous ceramic fibres with a melting, softening, decomposition or sublimation point lower than 2 043 K (1 770 °C) in an inert environment.</em><br><em>Technical Notes:</em><br><em>1.</em><br><em>For the purpose of calculating “specific tensile strength”, “specific modulus” or specific weight of “fibrous or filamentary materials” in 1C010.a., 1C010.b. or 1C010.c., the tensile strength and modulus should be determined by using Method A described in ISO 10618 (2004) or national equivalents.</em><br><em>2.</em><br><em>Assessing the “specific tensile strength”, “specific modulus” or specific weight of non-unidirectional “fibrous or filamentary materials” (e.g., fabrics, random mats or braids) in 1C010. is to be based on the mechanical properties of the constituent unidirectional monofilaments (e.g., monofilaments, yarns, rovings or tows) prior to processing into the non-unidirectional “fibrous or filamentary materials”.</em><br>d.. “Fibrous or filamentary materials”, having any of the following:<br>1.. Composed of any of the following:<br>a.. Polyetherimides specified in 1C008.a.; or<br>b.. Materials specified in 1C008.b. to 1C008.f.; or<br>2.. Composed of materials specified in 1C010.d.1.a. or 1C010.d.1.b. and “commingled” with other fibres specified in 1C010.a., 1C010.b. or 1C010.c.;</td><td></td><td></td></tr>
> <tr><td>e.. Fully or partially resin-impregnated or pitch-impregnated “fibrous or filamentary materials” (prepregs), metal or carbon-coated “fibrous or filamentary materials” (preforms) or “carbon fibre preforms”, having all of the following:<br>1.. Having any of the following:<br>a.. Inorganic “fibrous or filamentary materials” specified in 1C010.c.; or<br>b.. Organic or carbon “fibrous or filamentary materials”, having all of the following:<br>1.. “Specific modulus” exceeding 10,15 × 10<sup>6</sup> m; and<br>2.. “Specific tensile strength” exceeding 17,7 × 10<sup>4</sup> m; and<br>2.. Having any of the following:<br>a.. Resin or pitch, specified in 1C008 or 1C009.b.;<br>b.. ‘Dynamic Mechanical Analysis glass transition temperature (DMA Tg)’ equal to or exceeding 453 K (180 °C) and having a phenolic resin; or<br>c.. ‘Dynamic Mechanical Analysis glass transition temperature (DMA Tg)’ equal to or exceeding 505 K (232 °C) and having a resin or pitch, not specified in 1C008 or 1C009.b., and not being a phenolic resin;<br><em>Note 1:</em><br><em>Metal or carbon-coated “fibrous or filamentary materials” (preforms) or “carbon fibre preforms”, not impregnated with resin or pitch, are specified by “fibrous or filamentary materials” in 1C010.a., 1C010.b. or 1C010.c.</em><br><em>Note 2:</em><br><em>1C010.e. does not control:</em><br>a.. <em>Epoxy resin “matrix” impregnated carbon “fibrous or filamentary materials” (prepregs) for the repair of “civil aircraft” structures or laminates, having all the following;</em><br>1.. <em>An area not exceeding 1 m<sup>2</sup>;</em><br>2.. <em>A length not exceeding 2,5 m; and</em><br>3.. <em>A width exceeding 15 mm.</em><br>b.. <em>Fully or partially resin-impregnated or pitch-impregnated mechanically chopped, milled or cut carbon “fibrous or filamentary materials” 25,0 mm or less in length when using a resin or pitch other than those specified by 1C008 or 1C009.b.</em><br><em>Technical Note:</em><br><em>The ‘Dynamic Mechanical Analysis glass transition temperature (DMA T<sub>g</sub>)’ for materials specified by 1C010.e. is determined using the method described in ASTM D 7028-07, or equivalent national standard, on a dry test specimen. In the case of thermoset materials, degree of cure of a dry test specimen shall be a minimum of 90 % as defined by ASTM E 2160-04 or equivalent national standard.</em></td><td>M6C1</td><td>Resin impregnated fibre prepregs and metal coated fibre preforms, for the goods specified in 6.A.1., made either with organic matrix or metal matrix utilising fibrous or filamentary reinforcements having a specific tensile strength greater than 7,62 × 104 m and a specific modulus greater than 3,18 × 106 m.<br><em>Note:</em><br><em>The only resin impregnated fibre prepregs specified in 6.C.1. are those using resins with a glass transition temperature (Tg), after cure, exceeding 145 °C as determined by ASTM D4065 or national equivalents.</em><br><em>Technical Notes:</em><br><em>1.</em><br><em>In Item 6.C.1. ‘specific tensile strength’ is the ultimate tensile strength in N/m<sup>2</sup> divided by the specific weight in N/m<sup>3</sup>, measured at a temperature of (296 ± 2)K ((23 ± 2)°C) and a relative humidity of (50 ± 5)%.</em><br><em>2.</em><br><em>In Item 6.C.1. ‘specific modulus’ is the Young's modulus in N/m<sup>2</sup> divided by the specific weight in N/m<sup>3</sup>, measured at a temperature of (296 ± 2)K ((23 ± 2)°C) and a relative humidity of (50 ± 5)%.</em></td></tr>

> <tr><td rowspan="2">1C101</td><td rowspan="2">Materials and devices for reduced observables such as radar reflectivity, ultraviolet/infrared signatures and acoustic signatures, other than those specified in 1C001, usable in ‘missiles’, “missile” subsystems or unmanned aerial vehicles specified in 9A012 or 9A112.a.<br><em>Note 1:</em><br><em>1C101 includes:</em><br>a.. <em>Structural materials and coatings specially designed for reduced radar reflectivity;</em><br>b.. <em>Coatings, including paints, specially designed for reduced or tailored reflectivity or emissivity in the microwave, infrared or ultraviolet regions of the electromagnetic spectrum.</em><br><em>Note 2:</em><br><em>1C101 does not include coatings when specially used for the thermal control of satellites.</em><br><em>Technical Note:</em><br><em>In 1C101 ‘missile’ means complete rocket systems and unmanned aerial vehicle systems capable of a range exceeding 300 km.</em></td><td>M17A1</td><td>Devices for reduced observables such as radar reflectivity, ultraviolet/infrared signatures and acoustic signatures (i.e. stealth technology), for applications usable for the systems specified in 1.A. or 19.A. or the subsystems specified in 2.A. or 20.A.</td></tr>
> <tr><td>M17C1</td><td>Materials for reduced observables such as radar reflectivity, ultraviolet/infrared signatures and acoustic signatures (i.e. stealth technology), for applications usable for the systems specified in 1.A. or 19.A. or the subsystems specified in 2.A.<br><em>Notes:</em><br><em>1.</em><br><em>17.C.1. includes structural materials and coatings (including paints), specially designed for reduced or tailored reflectivity or emissivity in the microwave, infrared or ultraviolet spectra.</em><br><em>2.</em><br><em>17.C.1. does not control coatings (including paints) when specially used for thermal control of satellites.</em></td></tr>

Mixed Oxides of Nitrogen (MON);<br>Technical Note:<br>Mixed Oxides of Nitrogen (MON) are solutions of Nitric Oxide (NO) in Dinitrogen Tetroxide/Nitrogen Dioxide (N<sub>2</sub>O<sub>4</sub>/NO<sub>2</sub>) that can be used in missile systems. Having a worst-case rate stability equal to or better (less) than plus or minus 0,05 % averaged over 10 degrees or more; or<br>3.. A positioning “accuracy” equal to or less (better) than 5 arc second.<br><em>Note 1:</em><br><em>2B120 does not control rotary tables designed or modified for machine tools or for medical equipment. For controls on machine tool rotary tables see 2B008.</em><br><em>Note 2:</em><br><em>Motion simulators or rate tables specified in 2B120 remain controlled whether or not slip rings or integrated non-contact devices are fitted at time of export.</em></td><td>M9B2c</td><td>Motion simulators/rate tables (equipment capable of simulating motion) having all of the following characteristics:<br>1.. Two axes or more;<br>2.. Designed or modified to incorporate sliprings or integrated non-contact devices capable of transferring electrical power, signal information, or both; and<br>3.. Having any of the following characteristics:<br>a.. For any single axis having all of the following:<br>1.. Capable of rates of 400 degrees/s or more, or 30 degrees/s or less; and<br>2.. A rate resolution equal to or less than 6 degrees/s and an accuracy equal to or less than 0,6 degrees/s;<br>b.. Having a worst-case rate stability equal to or better (less) than plus or minus 0,05 % averaged over 10 degrees or more; or<br>c.. A positioning “accuracy” equal to or less (better) than 5 arc second.</td></tr>
> <tr><td>2B121</td><td>Positioning tables (equipment capable of precise rotary positioning in any axes), other than those specified in 2B120, having all the following characteristics:<br>a.. Two axes or more; and<br>b.. A positioning “accuracy” equal to or less (better) than 5 arc second.<br><em>Note:</em><br><em>2B121 does not control rotary tables designed or modified for machine tools or for medical equipment. For controls on machine tool rotary tables see 2B008</em></td><td>M9B2d</td><td>Positioning tables (equipment capable of precise rotary positioning in any axes) having the following characteristics:<br>1.. Two axes or more; and<br>2.. A positioning “accuracy” equal to or less (better) than 5 arc second;</td></tr>
> <tr><td>2B122</td><td>Centrifuges capable of imparting accelerations above 100 g and designed or modified to incorporate slip rings or integrated non-contact devices capable of transferring electrical power, signal information, or both.<br><em>Note:</em><br><em>Centrifuges specified in 2B122 remain controlled whether or not slip rings or integrated non-contact devices are fitted at time of export</em></td><td>M9B2e</td><td>Centrifuges capable of imparting accelerations above 100 g and designed or modified to incorporate sliprings or integrated non-contact devices capable of transferring electrical power, signal information, or both</td></tr>

> <tr><td>6A006</td><td>“Magnetometers”, “magnetic gradiometers”, “intrinsic magnetic gradiometers”, underwater electric field sensors, “compensation systems”, and specially designed components therefor, as follows:<br><strong>N.B.:</strong><br><strong>SEE ALSO 7A103.d.</strong><br><em>Note:</em><br><em>6A006 does not control instruments specially designed for fishery applications or biomagnetic measurements for medical diagnostics.</em><br>a.. “Magnetometers” and subsystems as follows:<br>1.. “Magnetometers” using “superconductive” (SQUID) “technology” and having any of the following:<br>a.. SQUID systems designed for stationary operation, without specially designed subsystems designed to reduce in-motion noise, and having a ‘sensitivity’ equal to or lower (better) than 50 fT (rms) per square root Hz at a frequency of 1 Hz; or<br>b.. SQUID systems having an in-motion-magnetometer ‘sensitivity’ lower (better) than 20 pT (rms) per square root Hz at a frequency of 1 Hz and specially designed to reduce in-motion noise;<br>2.. “Magnetometers” using optically pumped or nuclear precession (proton/Overhauser) “technology” having a ‘sensitivity’ lower (better) than 20 pT (rms) per square root Hz at a frequency of 1 Hz;<br>3.. “Magnetometers” using fluxgate “technology” having a ‘sensitivity’ equal to or lower (better) than 10 pT (rms) per square root Hz at a frequency of 1 Hz;<br>4.. Induction coil “magnetometers” having a ‘sensitivity’ lower (better) than any of the following:<br>a.. 0,05 nT (rms) per square root Hz at frequencies of less than 1 Hz;<br>b.. 1 × 10<sup>–3</sup> nT (rms) per square root Hz at frequencies of 1 Hz or more but not exceeding 10 Hz; or<br>c.. 1 × 10<sup>–4</sup> nT (rms) per square root Hz at frequencies exceeding 10 Hz;<br>5.. Fibre optic “magnetometers” having a ‘sensitivity’ lower (better) than 1 nT (rms) per square root Hz;<br>b.. Underwater electric field sensors having a ‘sensitivity’ lower (better) than 8 nanovolt per metre per square root Hz when measured at 1 Hz;<br>c.. “Magnetic gradiometers” as follows:<br>1.. “Magnetic gradiometers” using multiple “magnetometers” specified in 6A006.a.;<br>2.. Fibre optic “intrinsic magnetic gradiometers” having a magnetic gradient field ‘sensitivity’ lower (better) than 0,3 nT/m rms per square root Hz;<br>3.. “Intrinsic magnetic gradiometers”, using “technology” other than fibre-optic “technology”, having a magnetic gradient field ‘sensitivity’ lower (better) than 0,015 nT/m rms per square root Hz;<br>d.. “Compensation systems” for magnetic or underwater electric field sensors resulting in a performance equal to or better than the specified parameters of 6A006.a., 6A006.b. or 6A006.c.;</td><td>M9A8</td><td>Three axis magnetic heading sensors having all of the following characteristics, and specially designed components therefor:<br>a)) Internal tilt compensation in pitch (+/– 90 degrees) and having roll (+/– 180 degrees) axes.<br>b)) Capable of providing azimuthal accuracy better (less) than 0,5 degrees rms at latitudes of +/– 80 degrees, referenced to local magnetic field; and<br>c)) Designed or modified to be integrated with flight control and navigation systems.<br>Note:<br>Flight control and navigation systems in Item 9.A.8. include gyrostabilisers, automatic pilots and inertial navigation systems.</td></tr>
> <tr><td>6A007</td><td>Gravity meters (gravimeters) and gravity gradiometers, as follows:<br><strong>N.B.:</strong><br><strong>SEE ALSO 6A107.</strong><br>a.. Gravity meters designed or modified for ground use and having a static accuracy of less (better) than 10 μGal;<br><em>Note:</em><br><em>6A007.a. does not control ground gravity meters of the quartz element (Worden) type.</em><br>b.. Gravity meters designed for mobile platforms and having all of the following:<br>1.. A static accuracy of less (better) than 0,7 mGal; and<br>2.. An in-service (operational) accuracy of less (better) than 0,7 mGal having a ‘time-to-steady-state registration’ of less than 2 minutes under any combination of attendant corrective compensations and motional influences;<br><em>Technical Note:</em><br><em>For the purposes of 6A007.b., ‘time-to-steady-state registration’ (also referred to as the gravimeter's response time) is the time over which the disturbing effects of platform induced accelerations (high frequency noise) are reduced.</em><br>c.. Gravity gradiometers.</td><td>M12A3</td><td>Gravity meters (gravimeters) or gravity gradiometers, designed or modified for airborne or marine use, usable for systems specified in 1.A., as follows, and specially designed components therefor:<br>a)) Gravity meters having all the following:<br>1.. A static or operational accuracy equal to or less (better) than 0,7 milligal (mgal); and<br>2.. A time to steady-state registration of two minutes or less;<br>b)) Gravity gradiometers.</td></tr>
> <tr><td rowspan="2">6A008</td><td>Radar systems, equipment and assemblies, having any of the following, and specially designed components therefor:<br><strong>N.B.:</strong><br><strong>SEE ALSO 6A108.</strong><br><em>Note:</em><br><em>6A008 does not control:</em><br><em>Secondary surveillance radar (SSR);</em><br><em>Civil Automotive Radar;</em><br><em>Displays or monitors used for air traffic control (ATC);</em><br><em>Meteorological (weather) radar;</em><br><em>Precision approach radar (PAR) equipment conforming to ICAO standards and employing electronically steerable linear (1-dimensional) arrays or mechanically positioned passive antennae.</em></td><td>M11A1</td><td>Radar and laser radar systems, including altimeters, designed or modified for use in the systems specified in 1.A.<br><em>Technical Note:</em><br><em>Laser radar systems embody specialised transmission, scanning, receiving and signal processing techniques for utilisation of lasers for echo ranging, direction finding and discrimination of targets by location, radial speed and body reflection characteristics.</em></td></tr>
> <tr><td>a.. Operating at frequencies from 40 GHz to 230 GHz and having any of the following:<br>1.. An average output power exceeding 100 mW; or<br>2.. Locating accuracy of 1 m or less (better) in range and 0,2 degree or less (better) in azimuth;<br>b.. A tunable bandwidth exceeding ± 6,25 % of the ‘centre operating frequency’;<br><em>Technical Note:</em><br><em>The ‘centre operating frequency’ equals one half of the sum of the highest plus the lowest specified operating frequencies.</em><br>c.. > **7A**
>
> **Systems, Equipment and Components**
>
> <table>

> <tr><th colspan="2">The corresponding systems, equipment and components as identified in Council Regulation (EC) No 428/2009 of 5 May 2009 setting up a Community regime for the control of exports, transfer, brokering and transit of dual-use items</th><th colspan="2">Missile Technology Control Regime (M.TCR): Equipment, software and technology annex</th></tr>
> <tr><td rowspan="2">7A001</td><td>Accelerometers as follows and specially designed components therefor:<br><strong>N.B.:</strong><br><strong>SEE ALSO 7A101.</strong><br><em>N.B.:</em><br><em>For angular or rotational accelerometers, see 7A001.b.</em><br>a.<br>Linear accelerometers having any of the following:<br>1.. Specified to function at linear acceleration levels less than or equal to 15 g and having any of the following:<br>a.. A “bias”“stability” of less (better) than 130 micro g with respect to a fixed calibration value over a period of one year; or<br>b.. A “scale factor”“stability” of less (better) than 130 ppm with respect to a fixed calibration value over a period of one year;<br>2.. Specified to function at linear acceleration levels exceeding 15 g but less than or equal to 100 g and having all of the following:<br>a.. A “bias”“repeatability” of less (better) than 1 250 micro g over a period of one year; and<br>b.. A “scale factor”“repeatability” of less (better) than 1 250 ppm over a period of one year; or<br>3.. Designed for use in inertial navigation or guidance systems and specified to function at linear acceleration levels exceeding 100 g;<br><em>Note:</em><br><em>7A001.a.1. and 7A001.a.2. do not control accelerometers limited to measurement of only vibration or shock.</em></td><td>M9A3</td><td>Linear accelerometers, designed for use in inertial navigation systems or in guidance systems of all types, usable in the systems specified in 1.A., 19.A.1. or 19.A.2., having all of the following characteristics, and specially designed components therefor:<br>a.. ‘Scale factor’‘repeatability’ less (better) than 1 250 ppm; and<br>b.. ‘Bias’‘repeatability’ less (better) than 1 250 micro g.<br><em>Note:</em><br><em>Item 9.A.3. does not control accelerometers specially designed and developed as Measurement While Drilling (MWD) sensors for use in downhole well service operations.</em><br><em>Technical Notes:</em><br><em>1.</em><br><em>‘Bias’ is defined as the accelerometer output when no acceleration is applied.</em><br><em>2.</em><br><em>‘Scale factor’ is defined as the ratio of change in output to a change in the input.</em><br><em>3.</em><br><em>The measurement of ‘bias’ and ‘scale factor’ refers to one sigma standard deviation with respect to a fixed calibration over a period of one year.</em><br><em>4.</em><br><em>‘Repeatability’ is defined according to IEEE Standard for Inertial Sensor Terminology 528-2001 in the Definitions section paragraph 2.214 titled repeatability (gyro, accelerometer) as follows: ‘The closeness of agreement among repeated measurements of the same variable under the same operating conditions when changes in conditions or non-operating periods occur between measurements’.</em></td></tr>
> <tr><td>b.<br>Angular or rotational accelerometers, specified to function at linear acceleration levels exceeding 100 g.</td><td>M9A5</td><td>Accelerometers or gyros of any type, designed for use in inertial navigation systems or in guidance systems of all types, specified to function at acceleration levels greater than 100 g, and specially designed components therefor.<br><em>Note:</em><br><em>9.A.5. does not include accelerometers that are designed to measure vibration or shock.</em></td></tr>
> <tr><td rowspan="2">7A002</td><td>Gyros or angular rate sensors, having any of the following and specially designed components therefor:<br><strong>N.B.:</strong><br><strong>SEE ALSO 7A102.</strong><br><em>N.B.:</em><br><em>For angular or rotational accelerometers, see 7A001.b.</em><br>a.<br>Specified to function at linear acceleration levels less than or equal to 100 g and having any of the following:<br>1.. A rate range of less than 500 degrees per second and having any of the following:<br>a.. A “bias”“stability” of less (better) than 0,5 degree per hour, when measured in a 1 g environment over a period of one month, and with respect to a fixed calibration value; or<br>b.. An “angle random walk” of less (better) than or equal to 0,0035 degree per square root hour; or<br><em>Note:</em><br><em>7A002.a.1.b. does not control “spinning mass gyros”.</em><br>2.. A rate range greater than or equal to 500 degrees per second and having any of the following:<br>a.. A “bias”“stability” of less (better) than 4 degrees per hour, when measured in a 1 g environment over a period of three minutes, and with respect to a fixed calibration value; or<br>b.. An “angle random walk” of less (better) than or equal to 0,1 degree per square root hour; or<br><em>Note:</em><br><em>7A002.a.2.b. does not control “spinning mass gyros”.</em></td><td>M9A4</td><td>All types of gyros usable in the systems specified in 1.A., 19.A.1 or 19.A.2., with a rated ‘drift rate’‘stability’ of less than 0,5 degrees (1 sigma or rms) per hour in a 1 g environment, and specially designed components therefor.<br><em>Technical Notes:</em><br><em>1.</em><br><em>‘Drift rate’ is defined as the component of gyro output that is functionally independent of input rotation and is expressed as an angular rate. (IEEE STD 528-2001 paragraph 2.56)</em><br><em>2.</em><br><em>‘Stability’ is defined as a measure of the ability of a specific mechanism or performance coefficient to remain invariant when continuously exposed to a fixed operating condition. (This definition does not refer to dynamic or servo stability.) (IEEE STD 528-2001 paragraph 2.247)</em></td></tr>
> <tr><td>b.<br>Specified to function at linear acceleration levels exceeding 100 g.</td><td>M9A5</td><td>Accelerometers or gyros of any type, designed for use in inertial navigation systems or in guidance systems of all types, specified to function at acceleration levels greater than 100 g, and specially designed components therefor.<br><em>Note:</em><br><em>9.A.5. does not include accelerometers that are designed to measure vibration or shock.</em></td></tr>
> <tr><td rowspan="3">7A003</td><td rowspan="3">‘Inertial measurement equipment or systems’, having any of the following:<br><strong>N.B.:</strong><br><strong>SEE ALSO 7A103.</strong><br><em>Note 1:</em><br><em>‘Inertial measurement equipment or systems’ incorporate accelerometers or gyroscopes to measure changes in velocity and orientation in order to determine or maintain heading or position without requiring an external reference once aligned. ‘Inertial measurement equipment or systems’ include:</em><br><em>Attitude and Heading Reference Systems (AHRSs);</em><br><em>Gyrocompasses;</em><br><em>Inertial Measurement Units (IMUs);</em><br><em>Inertial Navigation Systems (INSs);</em><br><em>Inertial Reference Systems (IRSs);</em><br><em>Inertial Reference Units (IRUs).</em><br><em>Note 2:</em><br><em>7A003 does not control ‘inertial measurement equipment or systems’ which are certified for use on “civil aircraft” by civil aviation authorities of one or more “participating states”.</em><br><em>Technical Notes:</em><br><em>1.</em><br><em>‘Positional aiding references’ independently provide position, and include:</em><br>a.. <em>Global Navigation Satellite Systems (GNSS);</em><br>b.. <em>“Data-Based Referenced Navigation” (“DBRN”).</em><br><em>2.</em><br><em>‘Circular Error Probable’ (‘CEP’) — In a circular normal distribution, the radius of the circle containing 50 % of the individual measurements being made, or the radius of the circle within which there is a 50 % probability of being located.</em><br>a.. Designed for “aircraft”, land vehicles or vessels, providing position without the use of ‘positional aiding references’, and having any of the following accuracies subsequent to normal alignment:<br>1.. 0,8 nautical miles per hour (nm/hr) ‘Circular Error Probable’ (‘CEP’) rate or less (better);<br>2.. 0,5 % distanced travelled ‘CEP’ or less (better); or<br>3.. Total drift of 1 nautical mile ‘CEP’ or less (better) in a 24 hr period;<br><em>Technical Note:</em><br><em>The performance parameters in 7A003.a.1., 7A003.a.2. and 7A003.a.3. typically apply to ‘inertial measurement equipment or systems’ designed for “aircraft”, vehicles and vessels, respectively. These parameters result from the utilisation of specialised non-positional aiding references (e.g., altimeter, odometer, velocity log). As a consequence, the specified performance values cannot be readily converted between these parameters. Equipment designed for multiple platforms are evaluated against each applicable entry 7A003.a.1., 7A003.a.2., or 7A003.a.3.</em><br>b.. Designed for “aircraft”, land vehicles or vessels, with an embedded ‘positional aiding reference’ and providing position after loss of all ‘positional aiding references’ for a period of up to 4 minutes, having an accuracy of less (better) than 10 meters ‘CEP’;<br><em>Technical Note:</em><br><em>7A003.b. refers to systems in which ‘inertial measurement equipment or systems’ and other independent ‘positional aiding references’ are built into a single unit (i.e., embedded) in order to achieve improved performance.</em><br>c.. Designed for “aircraft”, land vehicles or vessels, providing heading or True North determination and having any of the following:<br>1.. A maximum operating angular rate less (lower) than 500 deg/s and a heading accuracy without the use of ‘positional aiding references’ equal to or less (better) than 0,07 deg sec(Lat) (equivalent to 6 arc minutes rms at 45 degrees latitude); or<br>2.. A maximum operating angular rate equal to or greater (higher) than 500 deg/s and a heading accuracy without the use of ‘positional aiding references’ equal to or less (better) than 0,2 deg sec(Lat) (equivalent to 17 arc minutes rms at 45 degrees latitude); or<br>d.. Providing acceleration measurements or angular rate measurements, in more than one dimension, and having any of the following:<br>1.. Performance specified by 7A001 or 7A002 along any axis, without the use of any aiding references; or<br>2.. Being “space-qualified” and providing angular rate measurements having an “angle random walk” along any axis of less (better) than or equal to 0,1 degree per square root hour.<br><em>Note:</em><br><em>7A003.d.2. does not control ‘inertial measurement equipment or systems’ that contain “spinning mass gyros” as the only type of gyro.</em></td><td>M2A1d</td><td>‘Guidance sets’, usable in the systems specified in 1.A., capable of achieving system accuracy of 3,33 % or less of the “range” (e.g. a ‘CEP’ of 10 km or less at a “range” of 300 km), except as provided in the Note below 2.A.1. for those designed for missiles with a “range” under 300 km or manned aircraft;</td></tr>

Components specially designed for equipment specified in 7A004.a. as follows:<br>1.. Optical heads or baffles;<br>2.. Data processing units.<br><em>Technical Note:</em><br><em>‘Star trackers’ are also referred to as stellar attitude sensors or gyro-astro compasses.</em></td><td>M9A2</td><td>Gyro-astro compasses and other devices which derive position or orientation by means of automatically tracking celestial bodies or satellites, and specially designed components therefor.</td></tr>
> <tr><td>7A005</td><td>Global Navigation Satellite Systems (GNSS) receiving equipment having any of the following and specially designed components therefor:<br><strong>N.B.:</strong><br><strong>SEE ALSO 7A105.</strong><br><em>N.B.:</em><br><em>For equipment specially designed for military use, see Military Goods Controls.</em><br>a.. Employing a decryption algorithm specially designed or modified for government use to access the ranging code for position and time; or<br>b.. Employing ‘adaptive antenna systems’.<br><em>Note:</em><br><em>7A005.b. does not control GNSS receiving equipment that only uses components designed to filter, switch, or combine signals from multiple omni-directional antennae that do not implement adaptive antenna techniques.</em><br><em>Technical Note:</em><br><em>For the purposes of 7A005.b ‘adaptive antenna systems’ dynamically generate one or more spatial nulls in an antenna array pattern by signal processing in the time domain or frequency domain.</em></td><td>M11A3</td><td>Receiving equipment for Global Navigation Satellite Systems (GNSS; e.g. GPS, GLONASS or Galileo), having any of the following characteristics, and specially designed components therefor:<br>a.. Designed or modified for use in systems specified in 1.A.; or<br>b.. Designed or modified for airborne applications and having any of the following:<br>1.. Capable of providing navigation information at speeds in excess of 600 m/s;<br>2.. Employing decryption, designed or modified for military or governmental services, to gain access to GNSS secure signal/data; or<br>3.. Being specially designed to employ anti-jam features (e.g. null steering antenna or electronically steerable antenna) to function in an environment of active or passive countermeasures.<br><em>Note:</em><br><em>11.A.3.b.2. and 11.A.3.b.3. do not control equipment designed for commercial, civil or ‘Safety of Life’ (e.g. data integrity, flight safety) GNSS services.</em></td></tr>
> <tr><td>7A006</td><td>Airborne altimeters operating at frequencies other than 4,2 to 4,4 GHz inclusive and having any of the following:<br><strong>N.B.:</strong><br><strong>SEE ALSO 7A106.</strong><br>a.. “Power management”; or<br>b.. Using phase shift key modulation.</td><td>M11A1</td><td>Radar and laser radar systems, including altimeters, designed or modified for use in the systems specified in 1.A.<br><em>Technical Note:</em><br><em>Laser radar systems embody specialised transmission, scanning, receiving and signal processing techniques for utilisation of lasers for echo ranging, direction finding and discrimination of targets by location, radial speed and body reflection characteristics.</em></td></tr>
> <tr><td>7A101</td><td>Linear accelerometers, other than those specified in 7A001, designed for use in inertial navigation systems or in guidance systems of all types, usable in ‘missiles’, having all the following characteristics, and specially designed components therefor:<br>a.. A “bias”“repeatability” of less (better) than 1 250 micro g; and<br>b.. A “scale factor”“repeatability” of less (better) than 1 250 ppm;<br><em>Note:</em><br><em>7A101 does not control accelerometers specially designed and developed as Measurement While Drilling (MWD) Sensors for use in downhole well service operations.</em><br><em>Technical Notes:</em><br><em>1.</em><br><em>In 7A101 ‘missile’ means complete rocket systems and unmanned aerial vehicle systems capable of a range exceeding 300 km;</em><br><em>2.</em><br><em>In 7A101 the measurement of “bias” and “scale factor” refers to a one sigma standard deviation with respect to a fixed calibration over a period of one year;</em></td><td>M9A3</td><td>Linear accelerometers, designed for use in inertial navigation systems or in guidance systems of all types, usable in the systems specified in 1.A., 19.A.1. or 19.A.2., having all of the following characteristics, and specially designed components therefor:<br>a.. ‘Scale factor’‘repeatability’ less (better) than 1 250 ppm; and<br>b.. ‘Bias’‘repeatability’ less (better) than 1 250 micro g.<br>Note:<br>Item 9.A.3. does not control accelerometers specially designed and developed as Measurement While Drilling (MWD) sensors for use in downhole well service operations.<br><em>Technical Notes:</em><br><em>1.</em><br><em>‘Bias’ is defined as the accelerometer output when no acceleration is applied.</em><br><em>2.</em><br><em>‘Scale factor’ is defined as the ratio of change in output to a change in the input.</em><br><em>3.</em><br><em>The measurement of ‘bias’ and ‘scale factor’ refers to one sigma standard deviation with respect to a fixed calibration over a period of one year.</em><br><em>4.</em><br><em>‘Repeatability’ is defined according to IEEE Standard for Inertial Sensor Terminology 528-2001 in the Definitions section paragraph 2.214 titled repeatability (gyro, accelerometer) as follows: ‘The closeness of agreement among repeated measurements of the same variable under the same operating conditions when changes in conditions or non-operating periods occur between measurements’.</em></td></tr>
> <tr><td>7A102</td><td>All types of gyros, other than those specified in 7A002, usable in ‘missiles’, with a rated “drift rate”‘stability’ of less than 0,5° (1 sigma or rms) per hour in a 1 g environment and specially designed components therefor.<br><em>Technical Notes:</em><br><em>1.</em><br><em>In 7A102 ‘missile’ means complete rocket systems and unmanned aerial vehicle systems capable of a range exceeding 300 km.</em><br><em>2.</em><br><em>In 7A102 ‘stability’ is defined as a measure of the ability of a specific mechanism or performance coefficient to remain invariant when continuously exposed to a fixed operating condition (IEEE STD 528-2001 paragraph 2.247).</em></td><td>M9A4</td><td>All types of gyros usable in the systems specified in 1.A., 19.A.1 or 19.A.2., with a rated ‘drift rate’‘stability’ of less than 0,5 degrees (1 sigma or rms) per hour in a 1 g environment, and specially designed components therefor.<br><em>Technical Notes:</em><br><em>1.</em><br><em>‘Drift rate’ is defined as the component of gyro output that is functionally independent of input rotation and is expressed as an angular rate. (IEEE STD 528-2001 paragraph 2.56)</em><br><em>2.</em><br><em>‘Stability’ is defined as a measure of the ability of a specific mechanism or performance coefficient to remain invariant when continuously exposed to a fixed operating condition. (This definition does not refer to dynamic or servo stability.) (IEEE STD 528-2001 paragraph 2.247)</em></td></tr>
> <tr><td rowspan="4">7A103</td><td>Instrumentation, navigation equipment and systems, other than those specified in 7A003, as follows; and specially designed components therefor:<br>a.. Inertial or other equipment, using accelerometers or gyros as follows, and systems incorporating such equipment:<br>1.. Accelerometers specified in 7A001.a.3., 7A001.b. or 7A101 or gyros specified in 7A002 or 7A102; or<br>2.. Accelerometers specified in 7A001.a.1. or 7A001.a.2., designed for use in inertial navigation systems or in guidance systems of all types, and usable in ‘missiles’;<br><em>Note:</em><br><em>7A103.a. does not specify equipment containing accelerometers specified in 7A001 where such accelerometers are specially designed and developed as MWD (Measurement While Drilling) sensors for use in down-hole well services operations.</em></td><td>M9A6</td><td>Inertial or other equipment using accelerometers specified in 9.A.3. or 9.A.5. or gyros specified in 9.A.4. or 9.A.5., and systems incorporating such equipment, and specially designed components therefor.</td></tr>
> <tr><td>b.<br>Integrated flight instrument systems which include gyrostabilisers or automatic pilots, designed or modified for use in ‘missiles’;</td><td>M9A1</td><td>Integrated flight instrument systems which include gyrostabilisers or automatic pilots, designed or modified for use in the systems specified in 1.A., or 19.A.1. or 19.A.2. and specially designed components therefor.</td></tr>
> <tr><td>c.<br>‘Integrated navigation systems’, designed or modified for ‘missiles’ and capable of providing a navigational accuracy of 200 m Circle of Equal Probability (CEP) or less;<br><em>Technical Note:</em><br><em>An ‘integrated navigation system’ typically incorporates the following components:</em><br>1.. <em>An inertial measurement device (e.g., an attitude and heading reference system, inertial reference unit, or inertial navigation system);</em><br>2.. <em>One or more external sensors used to update the position and/or velocity, either periodically or continuously throughout the flight (e.g., satellite navigation receiver, radar altimeter, and/or Doppler radar); and</em><br>3.. <em>Integration hardware and software;</em></td><td>M9A7</td><td>‘Integrated navigation systems’, designed or modified for the systems specified in 1.A., 19.A.1. or 19.A.2. and capable of providing a navigational accuracy of 200 m CEP or less.<br><em>Technical Note:</em><br><em>An ‘integrated navigation system’ typically incorporates all of the following components:</em><br>a.. <em>An inertial measurement device (e.g. an attitude and heading reference system, inertial reference unit, or inertial navigation system);</em><br>b.. <em>One or more external sensors used to update the position and/or velocity, either periodically or continuously throughout the flight (e.g. satellite navigation receiver, radar altimeter, and/or Doppler radar); and</em><br>c.. <em>Integration hardware and software.</em><br>N.B.<br>For integration “software”, see Item 9.D.4.</td></tr>
> <tr><td>d.<br>Three axis magnetic heading sensors, designed or modified to be integrated with flight control and navigation systems, other than those specified in 6A006, having all the following characteristics, and specially designed components therefor;<br>1.. Capable of providing azimuthal accuracy better (less) than 0,5 degrees rms at latitude of ± 80 degrees, reference to local magnetic field.<br><em>Note:</em><br><em>Flight control and navigation systems in 7A103.d. include gyrostabilizers, automatic pilots and inertial navigation systems.</em><br><em>Technical Note:</em><br><em>In 7A103 ‘missile’ means complete rocket systems and unmanned aerial vehicle systems capable of a range exceeding 300 km.</em></td><td>M9A8</td><td>Three axis magnetic heading sensors having all of the following characteristics, and specially designed components therefor:<br>a.. Capable of providing azimuthal accuracy better (less) than 0,5 degrees rms at latitudes of +/– 80 degrees, referenced to local magnetic field; and<br>c.. > **7B**
>
> **Test, Inspection and Production Equipment**
>
> <table>
> <tbody>
> <tr><th colspan="2">The corresponding systems, equipment and components as identified in Council Regulation (EC) No 428/2009 of 5 May 2009 setting up a Community regime for the control of exports, transfer, brokering and transit of dual-use items</th><th colspan="2">Missile Technology Control Regime (M.TCR): Equipment, software and technology annex</th></tr>
> <tr><td rowspan="3">7B001</td><td rowspan="3">Test, calibration or alignment equipment, specially designed for equipment specified in 7A.<br><em>Note:</em><br><em>7B001 does not control test, calibration or alignment equipment for ‘Maintenance Level I’ or ‘Maintenance Level II’.</em><br><em>Technical Notes:</em><br><em>1.</em><br><em>‘Maintenance Level I’</em><br><em>The failure of an inertial navigation unit is detected on the aircraft by indications from the Control and Display Unit (CDU) or by the status message from the corresponding sub-system. By following the manufacturer's manual, the cause of the failure may be localised at the level of the malfunctioning Line Replaceable Unit (LRU). The operator then removes the LRU and replaces it with a spare.</em><br><em>2.</em><br><em>‘Maintenance Level II’</em><br><em>The defective LRU is sent to the maintenance workshop (the manufacturer's or that of the operator responsible for level II maintenance). At the maintenance workshop, the malfunctioning LRU is tested by various appropriate means to verify and localise the defective Shop Replaceable Assembly (SRA) module responsible for the failure. This SRA is removed and replaced by an operative spare. The defective SRA (or possibly the complete LRU) is then shipped to the manufacturer. ‘Maintenance Level II’ does not include the disassembly or repair of controlled accelerometers or gyro sensors.</em></td><td>M2B2</td><td>“Production equipment” specially designed for the subsystems specified in 2.A.</td></tr>

> <tr><td rowspan="2">7D002</td><td rowspan="2">“Source code” for the operation or maintenance of any inertial navigation equipment, including inertial equipment not specified in 7A003 or 7A004, or Attitude and Heading Reference Systems (‘AHRS’).<br><em>Note:</em><br><em>7D002 does not control “source code” for the “use” of gimballed ‘AHRS’.</em><br><em>Technical Note:</em><br><em>‘AHRS’ generally differ from Inertial Navigation Systems (INS) in that an ‘AHRS’ provides attitude and heading information and normally does not provide the acceleration, velocity and position information associated with an INS.</em></td><td>M2D3</td><td>“Software”, specially designed or modified for the operation or maintenance of ‘guidance sets’ specified in 2.A.1.d.<br><em>Note:</em><br><em>2.D.3. includes “software”, specially designed or modified to enhance the performance of ‘guidance sets’ to achieve or exceed the accuracy specified in 2.A.1.d.</em></td></tr>

> <tr><td rowspan="2">9A112</td><td rowspan="2">“Unmanned aerial vehicles” (“UAVs”), other than those specified in 9A012, as follows:<br>a.. “Unmanned aerial vehicles” (“UAVs”) capable of a range of 300 km;<br>b.. “Unmanned aerial vehicles” (“UAVs”) having all of the following:<br>1.. Capability of controlled flight out of the direct vision range involving a human operator; and<br>2.. Having any of the following:<br>a.. Incorporating an aerosol dispensing system/mechanism with a capacity greater than 20 litres; or<br>b.. Designed or modified to incorporate an aerosol dispensing system/mechanism with a capacity greater than 20 litres.<br><em>Technical Notes:</em><br><em>1.</em><br><em>An aerosol consists of particulate or liquids other than fuel components, by products or additives, as part of the “payload” to be dispersed in the atmosphere. Examples of aerosols include pesticides for crop dusting and dry chemicals for cloud seeding.</em><br><em>2.</em><br><em>An aerosol dispensing system/mechanism contains all those devices (mechanical, electrical, hydraulic, etc.), which are necessary for storage and dispersion of an aerosol into the atmosphere. This includes the possibility of aerosol injection into the combustion exhaust vapour and into the propeller slip stream.</em></td><td>M19A2</td><td>Complete unmanned aerial vehicle systems (including cruise missile systems, target drones and reconnaissance drones), not specified in 1.A.2., capable of a “range” equal to or greater than 300 km.</td></tr>
> <tr><td>M19A3</td><td>Complete unmanned aerial vehicle systems, not specified in 1.A.2. or 19.A.2., having all of the following:<br>a.. Having any of the following:<br>1.. An autonomous flight control and navigation capability; or<br>2.. Capability of controlled flight out of the direct vision range involving a human operator; and<br>b.. Having any of the following:<br>1.. Incorporating an aerosol dispensing system/mechanism with a capacity greater than 20 litres; or<br>2.. Designed or modified to incorporate an aerosol dispensing system/mechanism with a capacity greater than 20 litres.<br><em>Note:</em><br><em>Item 19.A.3. does not control model aircraft, specially designed for recreational or competition purposes.</em><br><em>Technical Notes:</em><br><em>1.</em><br><em>An aerosol consists of particulate or liquids other than fuel components, by-products or additives, as part of the “payload” to be dispersed in the atmosphere. Examples of aerosols include pesticides for crop dusting and dry chemicals for cloud seeding.</em></td></tr>
> <tr><td rowspan="2">9A115</td><td>Launch support equipment as follows:<br>a.. Apparatus and devices for handling, control, activation or launching, designed or modified for space launch vehicles specified in 9A004, sounding rockets specified in 9A104 or unmanned aerial vehicles specified in 9A012 or 9A112.a.;</td><td>M12A1</td><td>Apparatus and devices, designed or modified for the handling, control, activation and launching of the systems specified in 1.A., 19.A.1., or 19.A.2.</td></tr>
> <tr><td>b.<br>Vehicles for transport, handling, control, activation or launching, designed or modified for space launch vehicles specified in 9A004 or sounding rockets specified in 9A104.</td><td>M12A2</td><td>Vehicles designed or modified for the transport, handling, control, activation and launching of the systems specified in 1.A.</td></tr>
> <tr><td>9A116</td><td>Reentry vehicles, usable in “missiles”, and equipment designed or modified therefor, as follows:<br>a.. Reentry vehicles;<br>b.. Heat shields and components therefor, fabricated of ceramic or ablative materials;<br>c.. Heat sinks and components therefor, fabricated of light-weight, high heat capacity materials;<br>d.. Electronic equipment specially designed for reentry vehicles.</td><td>M2A1b</td><td>Re-entry vehicles, and equipment designed or modified therefor, usable in the systems specified in 1.A., as follows, except as provided in the Note below 2.A.1. for those designed for non-weapon payloads:<br>1.. Heat shields, and components therefor, fabricated of ceramic or ablative materials;<br>2.. Heat sinks and components therefor, fabricated of light-weight, high heat capacity materials;<br>3.. Electronic equipment specially designed for re-entry vehicles;</td></tr>
> <tr><td>9A117</td><td>Staging mechanisms, separation mechanisms, and interstages, usable in “missiles”.<br><strong>N.B.:</strong><br><strong>SEE ALSO 9A121.</strong></td><td>M3A4</td><td>Staging mechanisms, separation mechanisms, and interstages therefor, usable in the systems specified in 1.A.<br><em>Note:</em><br><em>See also Item 11.A.5.</em><br><em>Technical Note:</em><br><em>Staging and separation mechanisms specified in 3.A.4. may contain some of the following components:</em><br><em>Pyrotechnic bolts, nuts and shackles;</em><br><em>Ball locks;</em><br><em>Circular cutting devices;</em><br><em>Flexible linear shaped charges (FLSC).</em></td></tr>
> <tr><td>9A118</td><td>Devices to regulate combustion usable in engines, which are usable in “missiles” or unmanned aerial vehicles specified in 9A012 or 9A112.a., specified in 9A011 or 9A111.</td><td>M3A2</td><td>Ramjet/scramjet/pulse jet/‘combined cycle engines’, including devices to regulate combustion, and specially designed components therefor, usable in the systems specified in 1.A. or 19.A.2.<br><em>Technical Note:</em><br><em>In Item 3.A.2., ‘combined cycle engines’ are the engines that employ two or more cycles of the following types of engines: gas-turbine engine (turbojet, turboprop, turbofan and turboshaft), ramjet, scramjet, pulse jet, pulse detonation engine, rocket motor (liquid/solid-propellant and hybrid).</em></td></tr>

> <tr><td>9B005</td><td>On-line (real time) control systems, instrumentation (including sensors) or automated data acquisition and processing equipment, specially designed for use with any of the following:<br><strong>N.B.:</strong><br><strong>SEE ALSO 9B105.</strong><br>a.. Wind tunnels designed for speeds of Mach 1,2 or more;<br><em>Note:</em><br><em>9B005.a. does not control wind tunnels specially designed for educational purposes and having a ‘test section size’ (measured laterally) of less than 250 mm.</em><br><em>Technical Note:</em><br><em>‘Test section size’ means the diameter of the circle, or the side of the square, or the longest side of the rectangle, at the largest test section location.</em><br>b.. > <tr><td>9C110</td><td>Resin impregnated fibre prepregs and metal coated fibre preforms therefor, for composite structures, laminates and manufactures specified in 9A110, made either with organic matrix or metal matrix utilising fibrous or filamentary reinforcements having a “specific tensile strength” greater than 7,62 × 10<sup>4</sup> m and a “specific modulus” greater than 3,18 × 10<sup>6</sup> m.<br><strong>N.B.:</strong><br><strong>SEE ALSO 1C010 AND 1C210.</strong><br><em>Note:</em><br><em>The only resin impregnated fibre prepregs specified in entry 9C110 are those using resins with a glass transition temperature (T<sub>g</sub>), after cure, exceeding 418 K (145 °C) as determined by ASTM D4065 or equivalent.</em></td><td>M6C1</td><td>Resin impregnated fibre prepregs and metal coated fibre preforms, for the goods specified in 6.A.1., made either with organic matrix or metal matrix utilising fibrous or filamentary reinforcements having a specific tensile strength greater than 7,62 × 10<sup>4</sup> m and a specific modulus greater than 3,18 × 10<sup>6</sup> m.<br><em>Note:</em><br><em>The only resin impregnated fibre prepregs specified in 6.C.1. are those using resins with a glass transition temperature (Tg), after cure, exceeding 145 °C as determined by ASTM D4065 or national equivalents.</em><br><em>Technical Notes:</em><br><em>1.</em><br><em>In Item 6.C.1. ‘specific tensile strength’ is the ultimate tensile strength in N/m<sup>2</sup> divided by the specific weight in N/m<sup>3</sup>, measured at a temperature of (296 ± 2)K ((23 ± 2)°C) and a relative humidity of (50 ± 5)%.</em><br><em>2.</em><br><em>In Item 6.C.1. ‘specific modulus’ is the Young's modulus in N/m<sup>2</sup> divided by the specific weight in N/m<sup>3</sup>, measured at a temperature of (296 ± 2)K ((23 ± 2)°C) and a relative humidity of (50 ± 5)%</em></td></tr>
